Formula Fang: Prescription [From differentiation] ⬧ Treat cold with warm, Treat Heat with Cold [Big symptoms]

differentiation.” ● Seven types of formulas (qi fang) ● Large, small, mild, urgent,
odd-numbered, even-numbered,

stop leakage and discharge. If EPI are involved in leakage/discharge conditions,
they should be eliminated first, or at least treated simultaneously in severe
cases. Failure to eliminate EPI first may cause them to be retained internally.

Etiology & Pathogenesis Formulation Principles
● Exogenous: 6 pathogenic factors, especially cold/heat, trauma ● Choose Blood-invigorating herbs as chief ingredients

obstruct qi flow resulting in phlegm/damp accumulation, and making blood ● For Blood stasis with cold, add warming herbs such as gui zhi & wu zhu yu
stagnation/stasis worse as well. ● Add qi-moving herbs to the formula as qi & blood are closely related
● Add blood-tonifying herbs to the formula because [Move & tonify]

● Invigorate Blood flow, reduce Blood stasis, move qi – Identify the cause, ⬧ 2. xu Blood itself is a common cause of Blood stagnation
location & duration of Blood stagnation; In acute conditions, the symptom ● Add Qi tonics to the formula because:
should be addressed, whereas in chronic one both the symptom & root should ⬧ 1. qi xu is a source of blood stagnation;
be addressed. ⬧ 2. blood-moving herbs that warm & disperse tend to consume qi

weak constitution, & the elderly. ● Invigorate Blood & reduce Blood stasis

Treatment Principle
● Acute bleeding: priority is to first stop bleeding & resolve disharmony later ● For bleeding from the upper jiao, avoid ma huang, gui zhi, sheng ma, chai hu
● Chronic bleeding: treat symptom (bleeding) & the underlying condition [ascending herbs (cid:0) disperse]
simultaneously ● For bleeding from the lower jiao, avoid niu xi, zhi shi, da huang, mang xiao
● Formulas include astringent, hemostatic ingredients as chief, and heat-clearing [descending herbs, purging]
or qi-tonifying or other ingredients to address associated sx/underlying factors. ● Dang Gui, Chuan Xiong, Dan Shen are often chosen to arrest bleeding d/t
● Use stop-bleeding formulas short-term blood stasis, to prevent blood stasis from forming as the stop-bleeding

[Excess pushes blood up (cid:0) guide back to where it’s supposed to be] already included

tonify Qi so that blood stays in vessels, raise Qi so that it doesn’t collapse down

## p.119

FORMULATIONS 2
Formulas That Treat Wind治风剂
● Wind syndromes are conditions involving signs & symptoms that present the Treatment Principles & considerations
characteristics of wind – ● External Wind: use acrid, dispersing & diaphoretics as chief ingredients to
● 1) wind is a Yang pathogenic factor that tends to disperse, moving upward & disperse pathogens from Surface
outward. It can therefore easily invade Yang, Exterior. ● When treating E-W, do not use sweet nourishing or sedating herbs since they
● 2) the nature of wind is to arise quickly, migrate & change rapidly. may cause EPI to transfer to the interior
● 3) wind tends to make things move. ● Internal Wind: use nourishing or sedating ingredients as the chief according to
● 4) wind is the initial exogenous pathogenic factor in causing disorders specific etiology
● ● When treating I-W, do not use acrid dispersing herbs since they may cause Lr
Yang to rise & impair Yin, Blood & Fluids, thus aggravating symptoms
● When treating I-W, herbs that invigorate & tonify Blood are often added since “
to treat Wind, first treat Blood. Once Blood is moving, Wind will stop by itself.”
● Because wind invasion is often associated with other pathogens such as heat,
damp, cold, or dry, it is important to address these
External wind Internal wind
Etiology External wind invasion Internal Zang-fu dysfunction
Zang-fu Skin, muscles, meridians, bones, Lu Lr, K
Symptom Chills+fever, HA, cough, itchy skin, joint pain & Tremors in the hands & feet, vertigo, hemiplegia,
stiffness convulsions
Tongue No significant changes Red or pale
Pulse Floating Wiry
Tr.Pr. Expel wind Extinguish / pacify wind
Relations External wind can generate internal wind, internal
wind may accompany external wind
Formulas That Dispel External Wind [疏散外风]
● Formulas in this section are primarily composed of acrid herbs that produce a ●
dispersing action, they must be used with caution & appropriate modification
in cases with injury to the Fluids or Yin.
● External Wind invades the body when the normal qi is xu, the interstices &
pores are open, or the protective qi is weak. Some of these formulas are used
in treating E-W that enters the flesh, meridians, sinews, joints & bones; Others
treat disorders in which E-W induces internal movement of wind

● Damp syndromes involve either an invasion of exogenous pathogenic damp or ● Damp is heavy, turbid & stagnant by nature. It tends to sink & stubbornly linger.
an internal dysfunction of fluids metabolism, i.e. distribution & transformation, Damp is thus classified as a Yin pathogen that tends to obstruct qi function &
arising from impaired SP (transporting & transforming), LU (dispersing, impair the flow of the body’s Yang qi.
descending & purifying) or KD (warming & transforming) functions. ● Sp is Zang most affected by damp.

● GI Endoscopes indicate amount of inflammation in GI system

location

(especially in the lower part of the body), loose stools

collaterals the extremities with no desire to drink, itchy skin, fetid leucorrhea

appetite, sweet or greasy taste in the mouth the joints, superficial edema

with damp epigastrium, diarrhea, anorexia, feeling of heaviness in the

Treatment Principles & Considerations
● Since damp conditions vary widely, treatment should be based on the nature & ● Damp with cold – herbs to warm Yang, dispel cold & resolve water-damp
location of the condition, the particular Zang-fu involved, and whether there ● Damp with heat – herbs to clear heat & dry damp
are other pathogenic factors complicating the condition. The specific Tx ● Water-damp accumulation – harsh herbs to strongly purge water-damp (only in
methods are: patients with a strong constitution) [ascites], short period of time
● Damp in Upper Jiao/Exterior: diaphoretic herbs to release damp from surface ● Damp with wind – herbs to dispel wind & expel damp [Bi syndrome]
● Damp in Middle Jiao: bitter &/or aromatic herbs to resolve damp, awaken SP ● Nei Jing: Open Ghost Door & Cleansing Palace
● Damp in Lower Jiao: herbs to promote urination & drain water-damp
Cautions & contraindications
● Herbs that expel damp are pungent, warming & drying on the whole. ● In cases where edema accompanies with chronic dz or pregnancy, tonifying
Damp-expelling formulas are consequently contraindicated in medium to herbs should be added to the formula to support upright qi, e.g. bai zhu, huang
severe Yin & Fluids xu, unless modified by adding fluid-generating, qi
dryness-relieving herbs such as hua shi & lu gen ● Add qi-regulating herbs to the formula because damp is a heavy, cloying
● pathogen that can easily generate qi stagnation
